# Owner list (lanes WD3 and WD4): the fields that stay open

Built 2026-10-04T19:31:27+00:00 by `scripts/remediation/fields/owner_list.py` from `C:/PythonProjects/AncientMap/output/remediation/fields/wd3`, `C:/PythonProjects/AncientMap/output/remediation/fields/wd4`. Owner decisions of 2026-10-01: a field no source supports stays empty, a point no source supports stays where it is - this is the list of both.

**3823 of 7114 classified questions** were put to the model, and this list is about those: a site a run never asked is not an open question of that run (each run's rounds say what it asked, in `ROUNDS.jsonl`).

## period_start (the field stays as it is): 2260

### no source could be found: 1443

| site | country | stored | open because | what was found |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Nagara (Ancient city) (`0fc4e372-5add-4a40-a51c-371e161e8bf4`) | Afghanistan | - | empty | No readable source gives a founding or first-occupation date for Nagara; Wikipedia's only date (2nd century CE creations at Tapa Shotor) concerns Hadda, and the Dionysus foundation story is legend. |
| Albanopolis (`5290a257-c16d-444a-8620-aa60ec6130da`) | Albania | - | empty | No source dates the start of Albanopolis itself: the city is attested only by Ptolemy (c. 150 AD) and a 1st/2nd-century AD inscription, which are mentions and not a foundation, and the identification with the Zgerdhesh ruins (founded 7th/6th century BC) is rejected by Maurer and Metalla, who find Zgerdhesh was abandoned under the Roman Empire, so Zgerdhesh dates do not date Albanopolis. |
| Justinianopolis (Epirus) (`0569a73d-875d-4d49-bc8a-74c9a7fa4704`) | Albania | - | empty | English Wikipedia says only that Justinian I repaired and moved the settlement, with no century or year; the Epirus (Roman province) article places Justinian's reconstruction programme after raids of 548-551 without dating this foundation; the only dated statement (6th century AD) is on Archiqoo, an aggregator site that restates Wikipedia and is not a usable source. |
| Olympe (`72ca2dcc-e6fc-44a2-93b1-c575e50929dc`) | Albania | - | empty | English Wikipedia dates only the fortification of the settlement (late 5th to early 4th century BC), not its start; Hungarian Wikipedia (citing Gilkes) says pottery shows the town was already inhabited in the 7th century BC (infobox: founded 7th century BC?), but that page states it in Hungarian only and no English-language page stating the start could be found, so no quote can state the start value; left for the owner (lead: -700). |
| The Lion Gate (`567bb6c8-7bd6-4bc9-aa27-f75ab92ea358`) | Albania | - | empty | The Butrint Project page dates only the lintel (Archaic or Archaic-inspired) and the reconstruction (6th century AD per Ugolini and Zheku, 1st century AD per Karaiskaj); the marker text dates the lion relief (may date as early as the 6th century BC, placed in the 5th century AD) and calls the gate Hellenistic with no year; no source dates the construction of the gate itself. |
| Aqueduc Maurétanien de Cherchell (`fac97fda-c33c-46eb-9c3c-e0afd48a2eab`) | Algeria | - | empty | Readable sources only say Roman era (fr.wikipedia) or Augustan/Jubaean time without a year or century (Encyclopedie berbere); the only dated source, romanaqueducts.info, returns 403 to plain requests and cannot be quoted. |
| Aqueduc Romain (`a85d081b-f87c-49fe-937e-3a13ce212d48`) | Algeria | - | empty | No readable source gives a year or century for the start of this aqueduct; Harba-DZ calls it only antique, Wikipedia only Roman. |
| Remains of Roknia (`4626dd74-00a2-4796-91b6-cc737f96d08a`) | Algeria | - | empty | French Wikipedia and Archiqoo give only the period word Neolithic, the Megalithic Portal 'Late Neolithic/Bronze Age', and the Encyclopedie berbere notes that origin and age of the dolmens are debated (Camps: not Neolithic, a coin of Faustina found in one tomb); no source dates the start by a year, century or millennium. |
| Roman Bridge of Mina, Relizane (`c211a54e-09f3-4a34-a50d-a00ab351c9a4`) | Algeria | - | empty | The 1st century AD appears only as a hedged statement in Arabic Wikipedia and on Archiqoo, with identical wording, no primary source; the Algerian heritage map's own record says the bridge dates to the Berber period (no year), so no reliable source dates the start. |
| Thibilis (`b9024baf-e614-498c-aa14-5c14d2c525b4`) | Algeria | - | empty | English and French Wikipedia give no start date; the Guelma tourism ministry page calls Thibilis an old Phoenician centre within Massinissa's Numidian state without a date, and the ASJP study likewise; the lone '1st century AD' line on Archiqoo contradicts that pre-Roman Numidian origin and has no primary source, so no reliable source dates the start. |
| Hartashen Megalithic Avenue (`08861937-eb56-4be0-9fef-778754fcfc1d`) | Armenia | - | empty | English Wikipedia states 'There is no firm dating of the monument' and only offers Neolithic or Bronze Age as a possibility (period words, no year); other web pages disagree (Bronze Age c. 2000-1500 BCE versus 6000-4000 BC), so no reliable start year can be quoted. |
| Karchaghbyur (`a4b56463-b8e7-48af-80d0-c01b5c9caba9`) | Armenia | - | empty | English Wikipedia only says a walled settlement near Karchaghbyur, 'of the Persian or Hellenistic period', was abandoned in the 1st century BCE; no source states when it began. |
| Aboriginal Sites of New South Wales (`d7b8c0a6-0e25-4c12-b63e-1e7abd9af00f`) | Australia | - | empty | The article covers many Aboriginal sites of very different ages (engravings of 5000-200 years ago, sites over 22,000 years old, others older); no source dates a single start for the entry. |
| Aboriginal Sites of New South Wales (`d7b8c0a6-0e25-4c12-b63e-1e7abd9af00f`) | Australia | - | empty | The places span the whole Aboriginal occupation of the state, from the earliest sites to historic ones, so no source dates one start for the entry. No source reached states it, so the field stays as it is. |
| Balls Head Reserve (`0dfe2efe-0699-4300-8f2e-04852c23ab55`) | Australia | - | empty | Heritage NSW and Wikipedia say only that the Cammeraygal used the headland for many thousands of years, with no year, century or millennium for the start of occupation; the only dated item (1926) is the public reserve's dedication, not the start of the Aboriginal site, and Bowdler 1971 is a scanned PDF. |
| Bidjigal Reserve (`8fe4115a-617d-4cfc-ab35-272e8b2c1db2`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia dates the oldest rock-shelter midden only as 10150 bp, a BP date that is not read as a calendar year; no source gives a year, century or millennium BC. |
| Bone Cave, Tasmania (`a9530765-9f35-44df-b2dc-2b7b029fee7f`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia dates the oldest material to around 29,000 years ago; sources give only years ago/BP, no calendar year, century or millennium. |
| Box Gully Archaeological Site (`29f84776-1d97-4e09-ad40-95ea6e2d7247`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia gives radiocarbon dates of 26,600 to 32,000 years BP (calBP) only; no calendar year, century or millennium for the start. |
| Carisbrook Stone Arrangement (`0e0531f8-6b94-43ab-b6db-81611a30e297`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia and the Victorian fact sheet give no year, century or millennium for the Carisbrook arrangement's construction; the fact sheet says its purpose is unknown. |
| Cloggs Cave (`0fc900b2-23a7-4664-ac47-0bce2852babf`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia says the cave was probably first occupied around 17,000 years ago; years-ago figures are not read as a year and no BC or AD date is given. |
| Cuddie Springs (`d6fabe8c-061f-4885-8e02-cc6276c6f23c`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia dates the Pleistocene layers only as 28 to 33,000 years ago; no calendar year, century or millennium is given. |
| Devil's Lair (`be5e8af5-aaee-4119-9572-a66ee691f455`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia dates occupation as 48,000 or 31,000 years BP only; BP/years-ago dates are not read and no calendar year is given. |
| Green Gully Archaeological Site (`dac9b6e1-a54d-4b9a-b400-5fa295ba2040`) | Australia | - | empty | Wikipedia, the Museums Victoria paper and the other pages checked give the Green Gully burial only as 'about 6500 years old' or by general years; no source states a calendar year, century or millennium BC for the start, so it stays empty. |
| Karta (`2ff6f6ff-dada-465c-ad3b-6db67b57a31b`) | Australia | - | empty | No source dates a site named Karta; the name is that of the island. |
| Keilor Archaeological Site (`68c4944b-9e3a-4561-955c-33fa9978fc86`) | Australia | - | empty | The Keilor sources (Wikipedia, eMelbourne, Victorian Places, Peter Brown) date the cranium and hearths only in years BP or years ago (about 14,700 and 31,000); none gives a BC year, century or millennium, and a BP date is not read. |

AD 600-800); Parsons' Middle Classic colony date (c. 400-550) is contested by Popenoe de Hatch's redating. No source dates the start of this site with a year. |
| Chitinamit (`835c08e0-d141-483d-b193-017a13a0e458`) | Guatemala | - | empty | The English Wikipedia article says only that Chitinamit dates from the Early Classic through to the Late Postclassic periods. Those are period words without a year, century or millennium, so no source dates the start of the site and the field stays empty. |
| Cotzumalhuapa (`b3b17110-a61a-43c1-8962-7c8f71947f52`) | Guatemala | - | empty | The English Wikipedia article says the Cotzumalhuapa zone dates mainly to the Late Classic period, was occupied since the Middle Preclassic period, and holds monuments ranging from Pre-Classic to the Late Classic (600-1000 CE). These are period words and a range for the monuments of the zone, not a year a source gives for its start. |
| El Baúl (`37999fa3-d22d-4c51-a2f5-fa0dd9d82942`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Wikipedia says only that El Baúl was occupied during the Formative stage (a period word); ORIAS dates the start of the whole Cotzumalguapa city, not of El Baúl, and Stela 1 (37 CE) is a monument date that may have been brought from Bilbao. No source dates the start of El Baúl itself with a year. |
| El Porvenir (`bdbce80c-eee9-4842-800a-1cf3e440c500`) | Guatemala | - | empty | The readable pages (Wikipedia) give no start date; the excavators' papers say only Preclassic/Middle Preclassic without a year and are not readable by plain GET. |
| Ixtutz (`aee27c1c-f426-4334-8e86-95dd2bf5c270`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Wikipedia and the excavation reports (Chaluleu 2019, Reyes et al. 2019) date the first occupation only as Late Preclassic, a period word without year, century or millennium; no readable source gives a dated start. |
| Kinal (`56f6f878-19b8-4098-8e60-347661302422`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Wikipedia dates only the major occupational phase to the Late Classic (c.600-900 AD) and a building program to the 8th century; no source dates the start (first occupation or foundation) of Kinal itself. |
| Kʼatepan (`e9e6da5f-9c61-444a-aa39-6a2a690813b7`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Wikipedia only says the main temple is of a style typical of the Postclassic (c. 950-1539 AD) in the Guatemalan Highlands, a stylistic range for a class of architecture, not a date for the start of this site; no readable source dates its start. |
| La Muerta (`b43e7f32-5f59-4370-9cf0-43c3f159e4e3`) | Guatemala | - | empty | English Wikipedia says only that La Muerta was inhabited during the Early to Late Classic Period; a period word without a year, century or millennium does not date the start, and no other readable source gives one. |
| Pajaral (`a996a5d6-dd44-441f-9616-b912c06f7f75`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Sources only give period words (Early Classic stela, Classic kingdom of Hixwitz, 7th century eclipse by Zapote Bobal), no start year or century for the site's foundation or first occupation. |
| Punta de Chimino (`dd8e1148-5057-4496-a859-5f28c798a3f6`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Wikipedia says only that the site was first settled in the Middle Preclassic period, a period word without a year; no readable source quotes a start year (the ceramic-sequence abstract giving 700 B.C. sits on a page the checker cannot read). |
| Sacul (`5df03f8d-0b22-4188-b7ae-ccd2b00d8f47`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Wikipedia gives only a main period of occupation (Late Classic) and the date of the cities' first sculpted monuments (AD 760-820), not the start of the site; the other pages only treat epigraphy of the Late Classic war and give no start date for Sacul. |
| Witzna (`eb13e438-eba3-44d7-8189-9365e245f0b8`) | Guatemala | - | empty | The sources found date only the 697 CE burning and a lake core going back to AD 300, not the start of the city; no source dates its foundation or first occupation. |
| Xultun (`47c3b2a4-e476-4d38-b390-280c136fd1c5`) | Guatemala | - | empty | The readable pages give period words only (tDAR: first settlements in the Middle Preclassic; xultun.org: Early Classic growth) and no year for Xultun's start; the Antiquity article that gives late Middle Preclassic (600 BC) is behind a bot challenge the checker cannot pass. |
| Zapote Bobal (`7d24037c-4afc-49e7-a13e-713769bf8208`) | Guatemala | - | empty | Sources date only the Late Classic kingdom (AD 600-900) and say the dynasty flourished about 200 years, disintegrating by AD 800; none states when the site was founded or first occupied, and the earlier-occupied periphery is undated. |
| Savaria Mithraeum (`626e8712-71b4-48ac-9b58-8440bade0fcc`) | Hungary | - | empty | English, French and Hungarian Wikipedia, mithraeum.eu and the Hungarian press report only that the Savaria shrine was in use until the 4th century (then burned and collapsed) and that it was found in 2008; no readable source gives a date for its construction. |
| Dhank Caves (`1d7cca0c-fb61-4b09-8692-52781474ed3e`) | India | - | empty | Wikipedia says the Dhank caves 'were carved in the seventh century A.D.' but also that they were cut under the Western Satraps (who ruled to c. 400 CE) and that Sankalia places them in the third century AD; the ASI says only 'Kshatrapa period', and other pages say 1st century AD. | Hadsar Fort (`fe61c9c4-97dc-4478-93f8-5cd4a44d8ec2`) | India | - | empty | Wikipedia says only that the fort 'is believed' to date from the Satavahana Era, a period word with no year or century; Castlopedia calls the construction date uncertain. No source gives a start year or century. |
| Jivdhan Fort (`4e7592eb-facc-4d84-a019-0d2f2311c1d0`) | India | - | empty | English Wikipedia says only that the fort 'originated during the Satavahana era' and German Wikipedia that its origins 'possibly' reach back to the Satavahana period while its present form dates from Shivaji's 17th century; these are period words with no year or century, and other pages give the Yadavas in the 13th century, so no start year is attested. |
| Khuntichi Vaat Hadsar Fort (`1a76bfc9-7056-4848-ac12-d24016a923bb`) | India | - | empty | No source dates the rock-cut steps themselves; Wikipedia gives only the period word 'Satavahana Era' for the fort as believed, and Castlopedia calls the construction date uncertain. |
| Sita Gufa (`3666230f-bc1f-4871-887f-db49556f9e3f`) | India | - | empty | The Marathi Wikipedia article says only that Rama and Sita took shelter here during their exile; the other pages give the Ramayana legend and no date for the cave. |
| Talaja Caves (`d321b4b1-0103-4190-ace4-125339800e75`) | India | - | empty | English Wikipedia dates the chaitya and cells to the 2nd century BC but in the same passage says the date of the caves cannot be traced and that some believe carving started by the end of the 1st century CE; the other readable pages are travel sites restating it, so no single start can be sourced. |
| Komplek Megalith Tanjung Raja (`e9eacdcd-80e7-4101-aa88-b942ec755f5c`) | Indonesia | - | empty | No source found dates the Tanjung Raja megalith complex itself; pages on the Pasemah megaliths speak of the region in general. |
| Megalitikum Rindu Hati (`069317ea-0971-47f0-acf2-306fb08a9ab2`) | Indonesia | - | empty | No readable source dates the Rindu Hati statue group itself; the pages found give only regional statements about the Pasemah megaliths, or no date. |
| Pagar Alam (`30cf4488-c74c-42d8-8e21-a017ca0bf921`) | Indonesia | - | empty | English Wikipedia only calls the megaliths around the city 'the remnants of a Bronze Age culture', a period word without a year, century or millennium; no source gives a start date for this entry. |
| Pagar Alam (`30cf4488-c74c-42d8-8e21-a017ca0bf921`) | Indonesia | - | empty | No source dates a start for this entry. The article only says the stone megaliths surrounding the town are 'remnants of a Bronze Age culture', which dates a class of monuments around the town and not this site, and the city existed as an administrative town inside Lahat Regency long before it became an independent city in 2001, so that date is no start of its history either. |
| Situs Megalit Tebing Tinggi (`cf3b581a-c47f-41c6-9922-48c6e3b8d3a0`) | Indonesia | - | empty | The Medialampung article, the Kumparan piece and the Pasemah papers describe the site only as a prehistoric megalithic site and give no year, century or millennium for it. |
| Situs Megalith Talang Kecepol (`bebaa4d3-fb8e-46a4-b9cb-fe6589925889`) | Indonesia | - | empty | The only dating on the Megalithic Portal page is the period label 'Neolithic and Bronze Age', a period word without a year, century or millennium, so no source dates this grave. |
| Dokan-e-Davood (`afbbd0e7-ca10-4aa7-942f-7d1293e3e79e`) | Iran | - | empty | Wikipedia says we have no certainty about when the tomb was built, and dates only the later reliefs to the 4th and 3rd centuries BC; Iranica gives only the period word Achaemenid for the tomb, so no source dates the tomb itself. |
| Ganjnameh Ancient Inscriptions (`3d801ecb-4453-4ce3-85db-dce392c67c9d`) | Iran | - | empty | Wikipedia, Iranica and Tehran Times only give the reigns of Darius I (c. 522-486 BC) and Xerxes I for the two inscriptions, a range that straddles the 500 BC bucket boundary and gives no year for the carving of the first one; the only 'about 500 BC' appears on travel sites, not on a source of record. |
| Nashtifan Windmills (`138007c5-f824-446e-84ad-cf44e7cfde76`) | Iran | - | empty | No source of record dates the windmills' start: the sources give only 'about 1,000 years old', a Safavid style label or 'unknown century', which are not a year, century or millennium for this complex. |
| Tomb of the Scene (`f927f3d3-7149-4786-94ac-412652b5123f`) | Iran | - | empty | The sources about the Sahneh rock tombs give only period words (Median or Achaemenid; Persian Wikipedia's infobox gives the 1st millennium BC in Persian only), no English-readable dated start. |
| Babylonian Theatre (`03f33939-7a7b-49f1-8ed1-99fb54897ae6`) | Iraq | - | empty | German Wikipedia says the founding date of the theatre is not transmitted ('Das Gründungsdatum des Theaters ist nicht überliefert, vielleicht wurde es schon unter Alexander dem Großen errichtet') and Livius only says it was built by Alexander the Great without a year, so no source dates the start. |
| Ardgroom (`f05255ff-a8b8-4a49-bb7a-f6fa9806445f`) | Ireland | - | empty | No readable source dates the Ardgroom (Canfea) circle itself: Wikipedia describes it without a date, and the Curious Ireland page only says that stone circles in general were built in the Bronze Age and are about 3000 years old (a class statement); the Megalithic Portal and Monumental Ireland refuse automated reads and the other pages are aggregator pages. |
| Beenalaght (`312d9851-989c-4b8d-9c24-094af1acc582`) | Ireland | - | empty | The Wikipedia article and the Irish Megaliths Cork page describe the Beenalaght (An Seisear) six-stone row without any date, and the Megalithic Portal and Kilshannig pages cannot be read automatically; no source dates this alignment. |
| Bohonagh Stone Circle (`9db1409d-3216-4448-b9c9-c42d5071ba4d`) | Ireland | - | empty | Wikipedia and Irish Megaliths give only the period word Bronze Age for Bohonagh, Fahy's 1961 excavation report says 'No datable finds were made', and the Roaringwater Journal's 1600-600 BC is a range for recumbent stone circles as a class; no source dates this circle's start. |
| Carnfree (`5928dcb7-1a73-4bf6-8bd3-3fe6ba8c6b90`) | Ireland | - | empty | Wikipedia calls the nearby mound only a Bronze Age mound (a period word, for the disputed Duma Selga), Voices from the Dawn says the Rathcroghan mounds 'likely date from the Bronze Age', and the Rathcroghan Visitor Centre dates only the medieval inauguration (1189 AD); no source gives a year, century or millennium for the start of Carnfree itself. |
| Carrigagulla (`b97f1e4c-09df-4190-aed5-22bfeb4a45cc`) | Ireland | - | empty | Wikipedia and Irish Megaliths describe the Carrigagulla axial stone circle and rows without a date, and IrishHistory.com gives only the period word Bronze Age for the five-stone circle type; no readable source dates the start of this complex. |
| Castlenalacht Stone Row (`c5d2dfc5-1a50-4b7c-a3f1-6879aa5b1c67`) | Ireland | - | empty | Wikipedia, Irish Megaliths, Trips.ie and IrishHistory.com only say the row is Bronze Age (period word, or "3,000 to 4,000 years ago", a class statement); no source gives a year, century or millennium for this row. |
| Cohaw (`d2ecae04-608d-4755-9546-cf9ec07547af`) | Ireland | - | empty | Wikipedia, Heritage Ireland, Baile Beo and IrishHistory.com call Cohaw Neolithic (period word, "roughly five thousand years ago" for court tombs in general) without a year, century or millennium for this tomb. |
| Gartnanoul (`fb877d92-953a-4dba-af74-64ac1330e47e`) | Ireland | - | empty | No source I could read dates the Gartnanoul court tomb itself. | Glantane East (`f32a3584-270b-43a7-9c12-859bb6214b11`) | Ireland | - | empty | Only folklorefinder.uk gives "c. 3000-2000 BC", an AI-style content farm page; Wikipedia, megalithics.com, IrishHistory.com and go-to-ireland give no year, century or millennium for the Glantane East complex. |
| Kealkill Stone Circle (`93a36090-1dd8-43e4-8da1-79782cb01b96`) | Ireland | - | empty | The only date for Kealkill is the Wikipedia note "This sort of stone circle is thought to date from the Bronze Age, around 1500 BC", a statement about the class of axial five-stone circles; other pages (Sheep's Head Way/Roaringwater) likewise give class ranges, none dates this circle itself. |
| Lia Fáil (`52ebea62-0e4b-4307-a6f5-03d06c3335f0`) | Ireland | - | empty | No reputable source gives a date for the erection of the Lia Fail standing stone: Wikipedia, Irish Megaliths and Heritage Ireland give none for it (Irish Megaliths says it was moved to its present place to commemorate 1798 dead), and the legendary and 5000-year figures concern the mound or myth, not a sourced date for this stone. |
| Magh Slécht (`94b971b9-ea1f-4879-8c25-bf59020f891a`) | Ireland | - | empty | Wikipedia says only that the area has been occupied 'from pre-4000 BC' (uncited, an upper bound rather than a start year); the Templeport study of the ritual landscape says only 'at least the Neolithic' with no year, and no source gives a start year for the site. |
| Templebryan Stone Circle (`c4bb47e2-6e2e-49e6-a17b-e9c2ad9c9129`) | Ireland | - | empty | No source dates the circle's construction. The English article describes only what survives and records nine stones standing in the eighteenth century; Megalithic Ireland likewise gives no period. | Tibradden Mountain (`b2a97f1e-1f85-4f7f-97d0-38da53be836a`) | Ireland | - | empty | Heritage Ireland and the Dublin Mountains Partnership call Tibradden Cairn a Bronze Age cairn (possibly originally Neolithic), and Wikipedia gives no date; all give period words only, no year, century or millennium for its construction. |
| Uragh Stone Circle (`31fabe04-8275-48fc-bdbc-85073302026a`) | Ireland | - | empty | Wikipedia and Irish Archaeology give only the 3300-900 BCE span of stone circles as a class and call the site Bronze Age; a visitor blog guesses circa 2500 BC for stone circles in general; no reputable source dates Uragh itself. |
| Adullam Grove Nature Reserve (`7b0e4de1-3212-46bc-b5fd-40ac1793faa1`) | Israel | - | empty | The reserve was declared in 1994 to protect woodland and contains several separate sites of different dates (Horvat Midras from the 10th century BCE, burial caves of the Late Bronze and Iron Ages at Hurvat Lavnin, Second Temple villages); no source gives one start for the reserve as a site, so a single year would be arbitrary. |
| Bet She'arim National Park (`b159a282-c65f-448b-9839-c1581d6a263f`) | Israel | - | empty | The sources give different starts for the site: Wikipedia says a first settlement dates to the Iron Age (no year), the town was founded at the end of the 1st century BCE and the necropolis was used from the first century CE; Encyclopaedia Judaica says settlement started in Iron Age II (no year). No single dated start for Bet She'arim as one site can be quoted. |
| Accua (`34182e34-cef8-4fe8-bcd0-bb1640be1cec`) | Italy | - | empty | Accua is known only from Livy's mention of its recovery in 214 BCE; that is an attestation, not a foundation or first-occupation date, and no source dates its start. |
| Arzachena Archaeological Park (`ec87f52c-4fd5-4be1-9754-494de52afb91`) | Italy | - | empty | Italia.it dates the park's eight sites only as a span from the 5th to the 2nd millennium BC, which does not state a start of its own in a form that can be quoted; the other pages date single monuments (Li Muri to the second half of the fourth millennium BC), not the park, so no source dates the start of the park itself. |
| Aufina (`c322cf91-d53c-4704-8c01-df7a0257a9a0`) | Italy | - | empty | English Wikipedia (Smith's Dictionary) gives no date for the start of Aufinum and Pleiades could not be read; Italian Wikipedia's infobox epoch (9th century BC) is unreferenced and at odds with its own text (excavated tombs of the 7th-5th century BC), and a local site speaks of settlement from the Neolithic, so no source reliably dates the start. |
| Cloaca, Capri (`6497dda4-f24a-40fa-92b5-85b16554d2fe`) | Italy | - | empty | The only source, Wikipedia, says the sewer was 'built in Roman times'; that is a period word without a year, century or millennium for its start, and no other source on this sewer was found. |
| Domus De Janas Torre Argentina (`0784c790-17b2-4450-9a95-286c1d9ef2f2`) | Italy | - | empty | No source found dates these Torre Argentina domus de janas; nuraghi.net only calls it an isolated domus with reuse, and the general date of the tomb type does not date this site. |
| Garden of the Fugitives, Pompeii (`6f6c5cc0-b0ac-4222-bb04-747352eb83d1`) | Italy | - | empty | No source found dates the start of the Garden of the Fugitives itself; Wikipedia and Pompeii in Pictures give only the 79 AD eruption victims and the 1961 excavation of the casts, and the plot is described as a converted vegetable garden and vineyard without a construction date. |
| Giants' Graves (`78123025-5f8c-433a-9623-a60ea4105600`) | Italy | - | empty | The sources date the class, not one site: the Italian article gives the giants' tombs a span of 1800-1100 BC and the tourism portal dates individual tombs (1800 BC, 2500 BC, 1800-1600 BC), so no source dates the start of this entry itself. |
| Grotta del Cavallo (`568aa67b-d4a2-46f0-91d8-ac0c0e820b75`) | Italy | - | empty | Grotta del Cavallo is a Palaeolithic cave dated only by periods (Middle Palaeolithic, Uluzzian) and by years ago; no calendar year or century is given for the start of its use. |
| Hybla Gereatis (`8423294a-2f28-4eec-87b0-9d55552cf023`) | Italy | - | empty | Wikipedia, Pleiades-linked pages and ToposText give only events (Thucydides, 211 BC revolt) and a vague 'Bronze Age to Early Mediaeval' range of remains on the hill at Paterno; none dates the start of the town itself. |
| Lupanar of Pompeii (`ee29e9ee-2892-4487-b318-0c0fc88f9a25`) | Italy | - | empty | No readable source dates the construction of the Lupanar at VII.12.18-20; English, German, French, Italian Wikipedia, Pompeii Online and pompei.it give only the 1862 discovery, the 72 AD renovation (German) and the 79 AD eruption, none a start date. |
| Megalithic Wall, Gubbio (`a4d1130a-68fb-4acd-8b77-5088ace9a377`) | Italy | - | empty | No source dates a megalithic wall at Gubbio. | Menhirs of Sardinia (`d7f5b63f-d9a6-41a4-b491-7ba11aa9cc96`) | Italy | - | empty | Donna Nuragica says the three Brentoni menhirs are only probably attributable to the Ozieri culture and that no stratigraphy or finds allow a firm date; a culture name and a class-wide range (menhirs of Villa Sant'Antonio made 3300-2500 BC) do not date this site. |
| Monte Bubbonia (`364ea265-5078-4181-a4de-46b51309996e`) | Italy | - | empty | Wikipedia dates only the ancient city's ruins (not before the 6th century BC), but the Beni culturali di Gela page and the Persee site notice report an earlier Bronze Age first occupation of Monte Bubbonia; no source gives that Bronze Age start as a year, century or millennium, so the 6th century is not the site's start and no start date can be sourced. |
| Palazzo a Mare (`d1877f2c-7bdb-46ee-8368-950eacc0edd5`) | Italy | - | empty | Wikipedia and Oebalus say the palace was built by Augustus (and later modified by Tiberius) without any year; the only figure is a 1903 Royal Geographical Society remark quoted in Wikipedia dating it 'about 27 to 37 A.D.' as a Tiberian villa, which contradicts the Augustan origin (before 14 AD, possibly BC) and falls in another bucket, so no source reliably dates the start. |
| Pantelleria Vecchia Bank Megalith (`daa1ea4b-c002-4552-a4ef-45d996c6cea6`) | Italy | - | empty | Origin is disputed (worked monolith of the early Mesolithic vs natural beachrock) and the sources only give years-ago figures or a period word, no dated start of the site. |

| Pozzo Sacro del Predio Canopoli (`cd66f88c-ac89-4f7e-9ef8-4bf167df259a`) | Italy | - | empty | Sources give period words only (Bronzo finale; fine Bronzo medio to età del Ferro) and Sardegna Turismo states the dating of the temple is uncertain between the Middle Bronze Age (16th-14th c. | Saticula (`7ef03e67-8439-4a61-84ab-c5e92424e17a`) | Italy | - | empty | English Wikipedia gives only the attack of 343 BC; Italian Wikipedia says the date of foundation is unknown and only speculates, conditionally, on a 6th-5th century BC Oscan origin. No source states a start for Saticula. |
| Segesta (`d83c15cf-679a-4e80-b138-344df541e860`) | Italy | - | empty | English Wikipedia states that the origin and foundation of Segesta are extremely obscure; the Italian article says the foundation date is unknown; park and tourism pages give conflicting occupation dates (11th, 9th, 8th century BC). | Selva di Malano (`a2e28715-eafa-4ac4-9bb3-1deaf34864b5`) | Italy | - | empty | The ArteCitta page gives only the period word 'Etruscan Roman period' for the villages, necropolis and rock monuments; no year or century dates the start. |
| Siponto (`7cfc73a7-180b-4378-a14a-785e9cab28a4`) | Italy | - | empty | Sources give a conquest in about 335 BC and a Roman colony from 194 BC (Ministry of Culture) or 189 BC (English Wikipedia), while the Daunian settlement is older and legend says Diomedes founded it; none dates the start of the site itself. |
| Somma Vesuviana (`b66e9572-4469-4d1a-b13f-b927af5d79db`) | Italy | - | empty | English Wikipedia says only that the city was founded by Oscans or by plebeians under Tiberius Gracchus, with no date; the Italian article dates only the later villa (late 1st century AD core) and events like the 79 AD and 472 AD eruptions. | Teatro Romano (`2a9a96d5-f85c-4ff5-b2ef-1e92c5c9b6ca`) | Italy | - | empty | English Wikipedia and the Regione Valle d'Aosta only say the Aosta theatre was built some decades after the city's foundation in 25 BC, in the late reign of Augustus, without a year or century; readable pages conflict (LoveVDA gives a first phase in the 1st century BC, other pages the 1st century AD), so no source dates the start unambiguously. |
| Tempio Nuragico a Pozzo di Irru (`ac3e720b-9c22-4757-ae62-6e29730c2dce`) | Italy | - | empty | The pages on the Irru complex (La Sardegna verso l'Unesco, Wikipedia on Nulvi, the list of Nuragic holy wells) describe the well temple without any date, and the only dates are for Nuragic wells as a class. |
| Tomba dei Giganti e Nuraghe Imbertighe (`1bb82113-962e-4782-9e83-9e3f19470949`) | Italy | - | empty | Italian Wikipedia, SardegnaCultura (Regione Sardegna) and the Archaeology Travel guide say only 'Bronzo medio' / Middle Bronze Age, a period word that does not date the start. The only years (1800-1600 BC) come from Pilgrim Map, a travel aggregator with machine-written text that cites no excavation or register, which I do not accept as a source. |
| Vescia (`1ac1779a-749a-4726-a671-d49a3d1b16e2`) | Italy | - | empty | The sources give only the city's role in the Latin/Samnite wars and its destruction (340 or 314 BC); none dates its foundation or first occupation. |
| Via degli Inferi - Necropoli Banditaccia (`a9dea7fa-8e85-4c6d-82c1-4c16b1a000b3`) | Italy | - | empty | Readable pages only date tombs and road works along the road (tumuli from the 6th century BC on the main sepulchral road, tombs and a road inscription of the 4th century BC); none states when the Via degli Inferi itself was made. |
| Virgil's Tomb (`c8d355dd-e6f9-44bd-b73b-a23f9e550590`) | Italy | - | empty | The readable sources date only Virgil's life and death (70-19 BC) and describe the monument as an opus reticulatum columbarium of the 'beginning of the imperial age' (a period word); none gives a year or century for the construction of the burial vault itself. |
| Ishi no Hōden Megalith (`a938f9bc-4f40-497d-8f2f-2ab44a0a2d1c`) | Japan | - | empty | English Wikipedia calls the monolith 'Of unknown age' and Japanese Wikipedia says scholarship has not established who made it, when or why; only the Harima Fudoki legend ties it to Mononobe no Moriya, which is not a dated start. |

A.D. 286); none gives a year or century for the start of the settlement. |
| Plain of Jars Site 3 (`3f7e973f-f972-4677-9d02-6ae0b6942fcd`) | Laos | - | empty | UNESCO and World Archaeology date the Plain of Jars sites only as a class (c. 500 BC - AD 500); the 1240-660 BC OSL date belongs to Site 2, not Site 3 (Hai Hin Lat Khai). | Haidara - Temple of Sun (`b3ebbc47-7624-4ef9-9f6f-bc03eab227f5`) | Lebanon | - | empty | L'Orient Today only places Haidara in the Roman period (Roman religious and funerary purposes) without a year or century for its construction, so no start can be quoted. |
| Hammarah Temple (`f0059dda-0b8c-4743-91ce-07d50c56ce1a`) | Lebanon | 1 | held | Sources call the sanctuary Roman and date only a reused architrave dedication (a portico of Ainkania) to between the mid-3rd and early 4th century AD, or the Hermon sanctuaries as a class to the late 2nd to end of 3rd century AD; no source dates the start of this temple itself. |
| Qasr Chbib (`60ae4c7a-9c8e-49cf-b85b-d49d8022787c`) | Lebanon | - | empty | The English Wikipedia article calls Qasr Chbib a complex of two Roman temples but gives no date, century or period for its construction, and no other readable source was found that dates it. |
| Qasr Naous Roman Temples (`b6516127-fbfb-4b63-895a-96e3b6c7dd2f`) | Lebanon | - | empty | Livius calls the two temples Roman and says only that the settlement north of them seems quite late; no readable source gives a year or century for their construction. |
| Roman Temple Temnin (`4e168b2a-9dd9-45ee-b569-c126d7e37faa`) | Lebanon | - | empty | No page found dates the Temnin el-Fawqa sanctuary or nymphaeum; the Wikipedia and Vici pages give only 'Roman' without a year or century. |

| Abbatija Tad-Dejr Hypogeal Complex (`6cf72484-5b8f-44b0-acfe-fd70ebfa5f26`) | Malta | - | empty | Sources date only later additions (four larger complexes 4th-9th c. AD, 13th-century chapel) and a period word (Late Roman/Byzantine); the early catacombs are undated, so no start year is attested. |

No source dates its start. |
| Għar il-Kbir (`dd6ec46e-813f-458a-a881-a5673ea8b839`) | Malta | - | empty | No source dates the first settlement of the caves: the article says troglodytes were inhabiting them until 1835, and the first written mention, of 1544, is a notarial deed and not a start. | Misraħ Għar il-Kbir (`efc21020-1705-481f-8e85-a2b1539ea6a3`) | Malta | - | empty | Wikipedia says the age and purpose of the tracks are uncertain, with origins estimated from the Neolithic to Medieval times; a no-later-than c. 700 BC limit and a Temple Period range for several examples are ranges for the ruts, not a start year for the site, so no source dates its start. |
| Salina Catacombs (`f10115c4-5ca9-4d69-990c-8054f9962ea3`) | Malta | - | empty | English Wikipedia only says the community that used the catacombs lived there 'in around the last half of the first millennium AD', a vague span about the community; Heritage Malta's Salini Catacombs page gives no date at all (only 'a Christian origin'), and no other readable source dates the start of these catacombs. |
| Ta' Kaċċatura (`e6b90e74-f7c5-40fa-846f-6ffab5ac8beb`) | Malta | - | empty | Wikipedia says only that the villa 'appears to have been built in the early Roman period' and was occupied in the Punic and Roman periods, and Heritage Malta gives no date; period words alone do not date the start, and the only year-like statement found is in a personal travel blog. |
| Tal-Barrani (`ad16f637-fa0c-4920-bfae-7a53dee2593a`) | Malta | - | empty | English Wikipedia dates only the 16th-17th century documents about the estate and says the Punic tombs and Late Roman-Byzantine catacombs were found there, without giving a start date for the site; a period word or the date of a document is not a start date. |
| Temple of Proserpina (`e7bb01c3-14d8-4dd1-9e63-5d804927a032`) | Malta | - | empty | Wikipedia says the date of construction is unknown; the 1st century BC or AD is only the date of a restoration (the Chrestion inscription records that the temple was already old), so no source dates the start. |
| Tombs of Malta (`ea25fd7b-6bae-4a45-8a75-60ef783a9230`) | Malta | - | empty | The entry is a group of many tombs of different dates across Malta; no source dates a start for the group as one site, so none can be quoted. |

No source dates the first occupation itself. |
| Agrour Amogjar (`3e25aa3a-9fb4-4e40-9989-07039d798515`) | Mauritania | - | empty | The British Museum African Rock Art page says Saharan sites in Mauritania are very difficult to date absolutely and only suggests the Amogjar paintings could be more than 5,000 years old (an age, not a calendar year); Wikipedia gives only the period word Neolithic. | Amazmaz (`e282f2a0-5fbd-445b-81dc-63432936feff`) | Mauritania | - | empty | Wikipedia gives only the period word Neolithic for the Amazmaz paintings, and the Vernet and British Museum material gives no calendar start; no source dates the start with a year, century or millennium. |

| Balcon de Montezuma (`c4d2251b-b64a-4615-a6f9-5a71ff6f3955`) | Mexico | - | empty | The INAH register says only that the site was occupied 'during the first nine centuries AD and from 1200 to 1500' (no start year), while Mexico Unexplained gives 400 BC, Spanish Wikipedia 400 AD and English Wikipedia a 600-900 CE build; the sources fall in different buckets and the authoritative one states no start year. |
| Chactún (`8e625e19-eeb1-4813-8244-14ec448d7440`) | Mexico | - | empty | English Wikipedia and the popular reports only date Chactun to 600-900 AD, which is its Late Classic florescence, not its start; the excavators' PLOS ONE report (Sprajc et al. 2022) says Middle and Late Preclassic material was also recovered at Chactun but gives no year for Chactun's own beginning, so no source dates the start of this site. |
| Cuatro Cienegas Footprints (`217b6292-d8ee-403b-a8ad-236304ac481d`) | Mexico | - | empty | No readable source dating the footprints was found. |
| El Tortuguero (`44def22f-dca7-4075-a555-d64821a0d882`) | Mexico | - | empty | English, German and Spanish Wikipedia give only a ruler's reign (644-679 CE) and a Late Classic period word for Tortuguero, and Spanish Wikipedia says few Preclassic remains were found, without a year or century; no readable source dates the start of the site itself. |
| Hoyo Negro Cenote (`82c9fb95-c776-4450-bb5c-93a70fdcb056`) | Mexico | - | empty | UC San Diego and Wikipedia date Naia only as 13,000-12,000 years BP (12,000 to 13,000 years old), a skeleton age in years before present that this field does not read; no calendar year, century or millennium is given for the start of the site. |
| Hoyo Negro Cenote (`82c9fb95-c776-4450-bb5c-93a70fdcb056`) | Mexico | - | empty | The dates given are ages before present: the source page puts Naia at 13,000-12,000 years BP and calls the assemblage a Late Pleistocene one for the region's cenotes as a class. | Isla de Sacrificios (`36778e23-1b9f-4094-a1cb-d3e1272e8d35`) | Mexico | - | empty | Spanish and German Wikipedia and the Real Mexico page speak only of Postclassic Totonac use and pre-Hispanic ceremonies on Isla de Sacrificios; no readable source gives a year, century or millennium for the start of the site. |

| Kulubá (`0f1ec894-b1b1-40e9-9e00-bd193b316484`) | Mexico | - | empty | The dates found (600-1050 CE in English Wikipedia, A.D. 600-900 and 850-1050 in Live Science and Spanish Wikipedia) are for the use of the palace only, not for the start of the site; INAH and others place first occupation in the Late Preclassic without a year, so no source dates the site's start. |
| La Florida (Zacatecas) Shaft Tomb (`e6b26bed-47b7-437b-97ab-60d88a018682`) | Mexico | - | empty | The only article on this tomb gives 'two centuries before our era and 400 after Christ's' for the societies of the area in general and says the chamber was unaltered for at least 1,600 years; that is a regional range, not a stated start of this tomb. |
| La Pintada (`4df3dcbd-62d3-4e37-92fe-e79989233f1f`) | Mexico | - | empty | Wikipedia gives only the peak of occupation of the camping grounds (700 to 1600 CE) and a hunter-collector use in the 12th and 13th centuries; the INAH pages linked are about Baja California caves, not this Sonora site. | Lamay (`6265475a-0d4e-45a7-b0fd-ad56fa0f4a89`) | Mexico | - | empty | INAH says only that Lamay functioned for a short time towards the end of the Classic period; no year, century or millennium is given for its start. |

| Miramar (`6a4de08f-3148-483b-8b5e-ac6ddbc1e8d8`) | Mexico | - | empty | No source describing a site named Miramar at this place was found, so no start date can be quoted. |

| Xtojil Cenote (`d217ef2a-7e8f-4ec6-8635-8c1970713899`) | Mexico | - | empty | No readable source dates the start of Xtojil; the leads (prominence in the 5th century, burials of 600-800 AD) date a phase, not the beginning, and could not be fetched and quoted. |

| Nakrang Tombs (`db0c6ae2-38d9-47b7-ae27-e6a960c0294e`) | North Korea | - | empty | Sources date the Lelang Commandery (108 BC to AD 313) and class the tombs as Han period, but none states when this tomb field began. |

| Fort Abbas (`008ecaa5-ce9b-484d-9eb8-0f27659bc91d`) | Pakistan | - | empty | The English Wikipedia article gives 1927 only as the year the older settlement of Pholra was renamed Fort Abbas, not as the start of the settlement: the same article says the place was known beforehand as Pholra and already functioned as a fortified outpost on the caravan routes, with Pholra Fort believed to predate the Islamic period. | Judeir-jo-daro (`24553e33-f5bd-409d-bf2f-04c193eb63cf`) | Pakistan | - | empty | Sources say only that the site belongs to the Mature Harappan phase (a period word); the DOAM chronology 3500 BCE - 1800 BCE is a generic span shared by many Proto Historic records and contradicts the Mature Harappan attribution, and no readable source gives a year for the start of this site. |
| Lakhan-Jo-Daro (`6dc58d5f-9c58-42da-831d-af82d9c07c94`) | Pakistan | - | empty | The only dated range readable by the checker is the DOAM 'Relative Chronology: 3500 BCE - 1800 BCE', which is a template for the whole 'Proto Historic' class (Mohenjo Daro carries the identical range); Wikipedia gives only the period word Mature Harappan and a radiocarbon sample date, and the Heritage of Sindh 2600-1800 B.C. page is behind a bot challenge. |
| Lohum Jo Daro (`8718faea-a442-445c-b369-c8572ec0be7e`) | Pakistan | - | empty | Wikipedia says only that the site belongs to the late period of the Indus Valley Civilization and Kingrani's page says it is an ancient site with Chalcolithic and Indus remains; neither gives a year, century or millennium, and the DOAM range 3500-1800 BCE is the template for the whole Proto Historic class, not a date for this site. |
| Lower Swat Valley (`3e0ef472-929c-4669-ad45-c7a19c78d25f`) | Pakistan | - | empty | The entry is a region holding sites of many periods (Aryan-era traces, Buddhist monasteries); no source gives a start year for it as one site. |
| Mulri Hills (`482a2a89-7c40-4f4d-a411-ab99de88b701`) | Pakistan | - | empty | Wikipedia names only the periods (Late Palaeolithic and Mesolithic) of the flint scatters found on the Mulri Hills; a period word gives no start year or century. |

| Moel Tŷ Uchaf (`ab1ccd8a-a3b1-4266-adf0-9c04b3e3b1f4`) | Wales | - | empty | No source I could read gives Moel Tŷ Uchaf a year, century or millennium. The English Wikipedia article describes it as 'a stone circle (but most likely a ring cairn)' with no date at all, and the descriptions that do date it give only period words ('early Bronze Age is likely', 'circular Bronze Age platform'), which the rules do not count as a dated start. |
| Moel y Gaer, Bodfari (`8ae2a431-14b9-4900-9b54-e8832b52cea0`) | Wales | - | empty | The records I could read do not date the start of Moel y Gaer, Bodfari. Coflein (NPRN 306682) reports only 'Period: Iron Age' and gives a terminus post quem of 410-200 cal BC for the beginning of rampart construction, which is a limit and not a construction year; the English Wikipedia article says the fort probably dates from the Iron Age but that there is no definitive evidence to establish the exact period of occupation. |
| Moel y Gaer, Llanbedr (`c6ad2aac-55ba-4cce-92eb-86f9e72c8781`) | Wales | - | empty | No readable source dates the start of Moel y Gaer, Llanbedr. Coflein (NPRN 306900) gives 'Period: Iron Age' and describes the fort as unexcavated since some digging in 1849, adding only that hillforts of this class were characteristic of later prehistoric settlement; the English Wikipedia article likewise gives no date. |
| Moel y Gaer, Llantysilio (`931d37ed-0a6f-4048-80ca-279f35f251e9`) | Wales | - | empty | No readable source dates the start of Moel y Gaer, Llantysilio. Coflein (NPRN 306813) gives 'Period: Iron Age' and describes building platforms identified by a survey, with no year; the accounts that do mention roundhouses give a mid 4th to 3rd century BC range for later features rather than a dated start of the fort itself. |
| Moel y Gest (`8d416cb3-e799-4990-96a9-50a70fde39c2`) | Wales | - | empty | The English Wikipedia article on Moel y Gest describes the summit fort but gives no year, only that it resembles the 'nuclear forts' of the Early Middle Ages, and Coflein's own record for NPRN 300956 (the stored source_url) lists the period as 'Iron Age' with a purely descriptive account. A period word alone does not date the start, so no source gives a year, century or millennium and the field stays empty. |
| Mynydd Illtud (`0c879c8d-da82-4297-be35-796650ca0765`) | Wales | - | empty | The Twyn y Gaer hillfort on Mynydd Illtud is called an Iron Age hillfort by English Wikipedia and by the Fforest Fawr Geopark page, and the Beacons NPA trail note says only that an Iron Age hillfort can be seen 'from about 2000 years ago', which is a relative age and not a stated year. No source states a year, century or millennium for the fort's start, so the field stays empty. |
| Pant-y-Saer Burial Chamber (`ba020c96-afeb-452a-8dbc-faf14155c96c`) | Wales | - | empty | Coflein's record for Pant-y-Saer Burial Chamber (NPRN 95529) calls it a Neolithic megalithic tomb reused in the Bronze Age without a year, and Anglesey History dates it only by pottery style to the 'Middle Neolithic'. Neither states a year, century or millennium for the tomb's construction, so the Wikidata P571 of -3000 remains unsourced and the field stays empty. |
| Parciau Hill Fort (`3f498fbd-0cb5-4910-8740-a4e2128f3b7c`) | Wales | - | empty | The sources for Parciau hill fort date only its Roman material, not its beginning: Wikipedia reports coins of Valerian (253-260) through Crispus Caesar (died 326) and notes that no finds have been attributed to the pre-Roman Iron Age, while Coflein's record (NPRN 95548) lists the period as 'Prehistoric, Roman' with no year. The Roman conquest of Anglesey, to which Waddington ties the abandonment, is not stated as a year for the fort's construction, so the field stays empty. |
| Pen y Gaer (`19ec6c34-0d5d-4a55-9c8d-d0cdda84b9ad`) | Wales | - | empty | English Wikipedia calls Pen y Gaer a Bronze Age and Iron Age hillfort and says the visible remains are mostly of Iron Age origin, and Coflein's record (NPRN 95289) lists the period as 'Iron Age' and the defences as incorporating several periods, but neither states a year. The only dated figure in circulation is an interpretive sign's '2300-1900 years ago' restated on a hobbyist page, which is a relative age, not a stated year, so the field stays empty. |
| Pentre Camp (`48a6d6b8-7224-4edf-b20c-a6fba056d8fa`) | Wales | - | empty | English Wikipedia calls Pentre Camp an Iron Age hillfort with a single undated sentence, and Coflein's record for Pentre Camp; Pen-y-Castell Enclosure; Pen-y-Gorddin Enclosure (NPRN 306750) lists the period as 'Iron Age' and describes the ramparts and phases of development without any year. No source states a year, century or millennium for the fort's start, so the field stays empty. |
| Roundton Hill (`003428cd-6046-41c9-a687-a8a2b392e19d`) | Wales | - | empty | No source dates the start of Roundton Hill. The English Wikipedia article says only 'There was once an Iron Age hillfort here', a period word without a year, and the Welsh monuments lists (the prehistoric scheduled monuments of Powys) give Roundton Hillfort only the class label 'Prehistoric' (MG337). Nothing states a year, century or millennium for its construction, so the field stays empty. |
| Slwch Tump (`5a5beef1-9bd1-472e-b77d-eefe6092cb06`) | Wales | - | empty | No source dates the start of Slwch Tump. Wikipedia calls it an Iron Age hillfort, the Coflein record lists the period as 'Early Medieval, Iron Age' and the Clwyd-Powys Archaeological Trust calls the hillfort 'probably representing an Iron Age tribal centre' - all period words without a year, century or millennium. The 5th-century Eluned of the legend is a saint's life, not a date of the fort. |
| The Gop (`9ed175c7-a706-4a71-b10a-f93a68734afc`) | Wales | - | empty | No source dates the construction of the Gop cairn itself. Wikipedia calls it a Neolithic monument and the Coflein record gives the period as 'Neolithic, Bronze Age'; the Flintshire monuments list says no internal finds were made and that it is dated to the Bronze Age while a Neolithic passage grave remains possible. The 'BC 400 TO 3000' in one account belongs to Pleistocene animal bones in the cave, not to the mound, and the 4170 BP figure is a radiocarbon date that cannot be read as a year. |
| The Larches, Monmouthshire (`752fc778-3443-43b9-9a4b-065fab448fd8`) | Wales | - | empty | No source dates the start of The Larches hillfort. The 'c. 800 BC - AD 74' range that circulates for this area belongs to Gaer hill fort at Trellech, a different monument, and a range for a class of forts would not count anyway. |
| Tregele (`5a3da9b9-6d74-4991-bfa3-3e41f600bcdf`) | Wales | - | empty | Tregele is a modern village and no source dates any ancient site at this point. The English Wikipedia article describes only the present-day settlement and mentions Llanfechell Cromlech a kilometre south, which is a different monument. No year, century or millennium is given for a start of occupation at Tregele itself, so the field stays empty. |
| Ty Mawr Burial Chamber (`e77b1f25-4675-472d-b63d-ff76fae4c08d`) | Wales | - | empty | No source dates the start of the Ty Mawr chambered tomb. | Tŷ Newydd Burial Chamber (`41eb7a6d-7eb0-4842-b25a-52cca7b3355e`) | Wales | - | empty | No source gives a construction year, century or millennium for the Tŷ Newydd chambered tomb. Cadw, the national monument owner, writes only that the chamber's layout 'suggests that it was built during the Neolithic (New Stone Age)' - a period word with no date for this site - and medievalheritage.eu attaches the generic span of the Neolithic as a class, '4000-2000 BC', not a date for this tomb, so the field stays empty. |
| Whetstones Stone Circle (`cfffa587-4250-43cb-89c2-84cad4e21c8a`) | Wales | - | empty | No source dates the start of the Whetstones stone circle itself. The English Wikipedia article gives only the general span of the stone-circle tradition as a class of monument, the Megalithic Portal records only that Burl thought the circle was destroyed in the 19th Century, and the landscape study notes there is no written record of the circle before that century - none of which is a construction year, century or millennium for this site, so the field stays empty. |
| Y Garn Goch (`454b824c-cc1a-4846-97d8-7c036712895b`) | Wales | - | empty | No source gives a year, century or millennium for the construction of the Y Garn Goch hillforts. The Brecon Beacons National Park Authority and Bannau Brycheiniog both date the occupation only relatively, as '2,500 years ago', which the rules exclude because a year worked out from an age in years is not read, and garngoch.org likewise speaks of 'about 5,700 years ago' for the stone circles on another site in the same group; the Welsh Wikipedia article adds no date, so the field stays empty. |
